</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 0.4em; padding-right: 0.4em; min-width: 8em;">Other</td>
<td style="width: 100px; border-left: solid 1px silver; border-right: solid 1px silver;"><div style="background:orange; width:3%; overflow: hidden;">&#8201;</div></td>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 1.2em; padding-right: 0.4em; text-align: right;">3%</td>
</tr>

</tbody></table>
</div>
</div>
<p>The majority of the population in El Salvador is <a href="/wiki/Christians" title="Christians">Christian</a>. <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Roman Catholics</a> (47%) and <a href="/wiki/Protestantism" title="Protestantism">Protestants</a> (33%) are the two major religious groups in the country, with the Catholic Church the largest denomination.<sup id="cite_ref-IRFR2012_164-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IRFR2012-164">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> Those not affiliated with any religious group amount to 17% of the population.<sup id="cite_ref-IRFR2012_164-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IRFR2012-164">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> The remainder of the population (3%) is made up of <a href="/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses" title="Jehovah&#39;s Witnesses">Jehovah's Witnesses</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hare_Krishnas" class="mw-redirect" title="Hare Krishnas">Hare Krishnas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Islam_in_El_Salvador" title="Islam in El Salvador">Muslims</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jews" title="Jews">Jews</a>, <a href="/wiki/Buddhists" class="mw-redirect" title="Buddhists">Buddhists</a>, <a href="/wiki/Latter-day_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Latter-day Saints">Latter-day Saints</a>, and those adhering to indigenous religious beliefs.<sup id="cite_ref-IRFR2012_164-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IRFR2012-164">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> The number of evangelicals in the country is growing rapidly.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/%C3%93scar_Romero" title="Óscar Romero">Óscar Romero</a>, the first Salvadoran saint, was canonized by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> on 14 October 2018.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=El_Salvador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_El_Salvador" title="Education in El Salvador">Education in El Salvador</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Asociaci%C3%B3n_Salvadore%C3%B1a_de_Astronom%C3%ADa_Observatorio_%22Dr._Prudencio_Llach%22.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Asociaci%C3%B3n_Salvadore%C3%B1a_de_Astronom%C3%ADa_Observatorio_%22Dr._Prudencio_Llach%22.jpg/220px-Asociaci%C3%B3n_Salvadore%C3%B1a_de_Astronom%C3%ADa_Observatorio_%22Dr._Prudencio_Llach%22.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Asociaci%C3%B3n_Salvadore%C3%B1a_de_Astronom%C3%ADa_Observatorio_%22Dr._Prudencio_Llach%22.jpg/330px-Asociaci%C3%B3n_Salvadore%C3%B1a_de_Astronom%C3%ADa_Observatorio_%22Dr._Prudencio_Llach%22.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Asociaci%C3%B3n_Salvadore%C3%B1a_de_Astronom%C3%ADa_Observatorio_%22Dr._Prudencio_Llach%22.jpg/440px-Asociaci%C3%B3n_Salvadore%C3%B1a_de_Astronom%C3%ADa_Observatorio_%22Dr._Prudencio_Llach%22.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6016" data-file-height="4000" /></a><figcaption>Dr. Prudencio Llach Observatory</figcaption></figure>
<p>The public education system in El Salvador is severely lacking in resources. Class sizes in public schools can be as large as 50 children per classroom. Salvadorans who can afford the cost often choose to send their children to private schools, which are regarded as being better-quality than public schools. Most private schools follow American, European or other advanced systems. Lower-income families are forced to rely on public education.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Education in El Salvador is free through high school. After nine years of basic education (elementary–middle school), students have the option of a two-year high school or a three-year high school. A two-year high school prepares the student for transfer to a university. A three-year high school allows the student to graduate and enter the workforce in a vocational career, or to transfer to a university to further their education in their chosen field.<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/List_of_universities_in_El_Salvador" title="List of universities in El Salvador">Universities in El Salvador</a> include a central public institution, the <a href="/wiki/Universidad_de_El_Salvador" class="mw-redirect" title="Universidad de El Salvador">Universidad de El Salvador</a>, and many other specialized private universities. El Salvador was ranked 96th in the <a href="/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index" title="Global Innovation Index">Global Innovation Index</a> in 2021, up from 108th in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Crime">Crime</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=El_Salvador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Crime">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Crime_in_El_Salvador" title="Crime in El Salvador">Crime in El Salvador</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Drone_PNC_El_Salvador.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Drone_PNC_El_Salvador.jpg/220px-Drone_PNC_El_Salvador.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Drone_PNC_El_Salvador.jpg/330px-Drone_PNC_El_Salvador.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Drone_PNC_El_Salvador.jpg/440px-Drone_PNC_El_Salvador.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1067" /></a><figcaption>Drone for unmanned aerial patrols</figcaption></figure>
<p>Since the early twenty-first century, El Salvador has experienced high crime rates, including gang-related crimes and <a href="/wiki/Juvenile_delinquency" title="Juvenile delinquency">juvenile delinquency</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> El Salvador had the highest murder rate in the world in 2012 but experienced a sharp decline in 2019 with a new centrist government in power.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> It is also considered an epicentre of a gang crisis, along with Guatemala and Honduras.<sup id="cite_ref-FAS_175-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FAS-175">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> Several journalistic investigations indicate that the government administrations of Carlos Mauricio Funes Cartagena<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> and Salvador Sánchez Cerén,<sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> far from working to eradicate violence and the actions of gang groups, made truces with the gangs Barrio 18 and Mara Salvatrucha<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> to keep a certain control over criminal activities and murders in the Salvadoran territory. In response to this, the government has set up countless programs to try to guide the youth away from gang membership; so far its efforts have not produced any quick results. One of the government programs was a gang reform called "<a href="/wiki/La_Mano_Dura" title="La Mano Dura">Super Mano Dura</a>" (Super Firm Hand). Super Mano Dura had little success and was highly criticized by the United Nations. It experienced temporary success in 2004 but there was a rise in crime after 2005. In 2004, there were 41 intentional homicides per 100,000 citizens, with 60% of the homicides committed being gang-related.<sup id="cite_ref-FAS_175-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FAS-175">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> In 2012, the homicide rate had increased to 66 per 100,000 inhabitants, more than triple the rate in Mexico.<sup id="cite_ref-NYRB_179-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYRB-179">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> In 2011 there were an estimated 25,000 gang members at large in El Salvador, with another 9,000 in prison.<sup id="cite_ref-NYRB_179-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYRB-179">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> The most well-known gangs, called "<a href="/wiki/Mara_(gang)" title="Mara (gang)">maras</a>" in colloquial Spanish, are <a href="/wiki/MS-13" title="MS-13">Mara Salvatrucha</a> and their rivals <a href="/wiki/18th_Street_gang" title="18th Street gang">Barrio 18</a>. Maras are hunted by <a href="/wiki/Death_squad" title="Death squad">death squads</a> including <a href="/wiki/Sombra_Negra" title="Sombra Negra">Sombra Negra</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2015, there were 6,650 homicides recorded in El Salvador.<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> In 2016, at least 5,728 people were murdered.<sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> In 2017, there were 3,962 recorded homicides.<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup> In 2018, there were 3,348 recorded deaths.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> In 2019, authorities reported a total of 2,365 homicides.<sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> In 2020, there were only 1,322 reported homicides.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> In 2021, the country recorded 1,140 homicides.<sup id="cite_ref-188" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-188">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> According to official data, 2021 had the lowest number recorded murders since the end of the civil war in 1992.
</p><p>By 2022, El Salvador had a homicide rate of 7.8 per 100,000 individuals.<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 10 May 2023, President Nayib Bukele stated on Twitter that El Salvador had completed one full year or 365 days since 2019 without a single homicide occurring. This announcement was accompanied by a video detailing his government's claims about this dramatic change in the occurrences of homicide.<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="2022_Crackdown_on_gangs">2022 Crackdown on gangs</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=El_Salvador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: 2022 Crackdown on gangs">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/2022_Salvadoran_gang_crackdown" class="mw-redirect" title="2022 Salvadoran gang crackdown">2022 Salvadoran gang crackdown</a></div>
<p>Beginning on 25 March 2022, three days of gang-related violence occurred that left 87 people dead.<sup id="cite_ref-france24.com_191-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-france24.com-191">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> In response, President Bukele asked the Salvadoran parliament to ratify a state of emergency.<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> On 26 March, Bukele also ordered the police and army to initiate mass-arrests against those responsible for the violence.
</p><p>A day later, <a href="/wiki/Legislative_Assembly_of_El_Salvador" title="Legislative Assembly of El Salvador">Congress</a> approved a "<a href="/wiki/State_of_emergency" title="State of emergency">State of Emergency</a>" that gives legal coverage to arrest any citizen suspected to be a gang member even with no proof. In addition, Congress also approved reforms to increase the maximum sentence for gang member from nine to 45 years in prison and punish the dissemination of gang messages, including independent journalism talking about the gang crisis, with up to 15 years in prison.
</p><p>The law was directed against those who "mark" their territories with acronyms of the gangs, a practice that gang members use to intimidate, and threaten with death those who denounce them to the authorities. The Directorate of Penal Centers began to erase the graffiti that the gangs use to mark the territory in which they operate.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/MS-13" title="MS-13">Mara Salvatrucha (MS-13)</a> and <a href="/wiki/18th_Street_gang" title="18th Street gang">Barrio 18 gangs</a>, among others, were estimated in 2022 to have around some 70,000 members, and as of August 2023, around 72,000 suspected gang members have been sent to prison as a part of the government crackdown on the gangs.<sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=El_Salvador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_El_Salvador" title="Culture of El Salvador">Culture of El Salvador</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:SalvadorDelMundo.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/SalvadorDelMundo.jpg/170px-SalvadorDelMundo.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="266" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/SalvadorDelMundo.jpg/255px-SalvadorDelMundo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/SalvadorDelMundo.jpg/340px-SalvadorDelMundo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="409" data-file-height="640" /></a><figcaption>The iconic statue of Christ on the globe sphere of planet earth is part of the <a href="/wiki/Monumento_al_Divino_Salvador_del_Mundo" title="Monumento al Divino Salvador del Mundo">Monumento al Divino Salvador del Mundo</a> ('Monument to the Divine Saviour of the World') on Plaza El Salvador del Mundo ('The Saviour of the World Plaza'), a landmark located in the country's capital, San Salvador.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Pulling from indigenous, colonial Spanish and African influences, a composite population was formed as a result of intermarrying between the natives, European settlers, and enslaved Africans. The Catholic Church plays an important role in the Salvadoran culture. Archbishop <a href="/wiki/%C3%93scar_Romero" title="Óscar Romero">Óscar Romero</a> is a national hero for his role in resisting human rights violations that were occurring in the lead-up to the Salvadoran Civil War.<sup id="cite_ref-Eaton_194-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eaton-194">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> Significant foreign personalities in El Salvador were the Jesuit priests and professors <a href="/wiki/Ignacio_Ellacur%C3%ADa" title="Ignacio Ellacuría">Ignacio Ellacuría</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ignacio_Mart%C3%ADn-Bar%C3%B3" title="Ignacio Martín-Baró">Ignacio Martín-Baró</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Segundo_Montes" title="Segundo Montes">Segundo Montes</a>, who were murdered in 1989 by the Salvadoran Army during the height of the civil war.
</p><p>Painting, ceramics and textiles are the principal manual artistic mediums. Writers <a href="/wiki/Francisco_Gavidia" title="Francisco Gavidia">Francisco Gavidia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Salarru%C3%A9" title="Salarrué">Salarrué</a> (Salvador Salazar Arrué), <a href="/wiki/Claudia_Lars" title="Claudia Lars">Claudia Lars</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alfredo_Espino" title="Alfredo Espino">Alfredo Espino</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pedro_Geoffroy_Rivas" title="Pedro Geoffroy Rivas">Pedro Geoffroy Rivas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Manlio_Argueta" title="Manlio Argueta">Manlio Argueta</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Roberto_Cea" title="José Roberto Cea">José Roberto Cea</a>, and poet <a href="/wiki/Roque_Dalton" title="Roque Dalton">Roque Dalton</a> are important writers from El Salvador. Notable 20th-century personages include the late filmmaker Baltasar Polio, female film director <a href="/wiki/Patricia_Chica" title="Patricia Chica">Patricia Chica</a>, artist <a href="/wiki/Fernando_Llort" title="Fernando Llort">Fernando Llort</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Caricaturist" class="mw-redirect" title="Caricaturist">caricaturist</a> <a href="/wiki/To%C3%B1o_Salazar" title="Toño Salazar">Toño Salazar</a>.
</p><p>Among the more renowned representatives of the graphic arts are the painters <a href="/wiki/Augusto_Crespin" title="Augusto Crespin">Augusto Crespin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Noe_Canjura" title="Noe Canjura">Noe Canjura</a>, <a href="/wiki/Carlos_Ca%C3%B1as" title="Carlos Cañas">Carlos Cañas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Gil" title="Giovanni Gil">Giovanni Gil</a>, Julia Díaz, Mauricio Mejia, Maria Elena Palomo de Mejia, <a href="/wiki/Camilo_Minero" title="Camilo Minero">Camilo Minero</a>, Ricardo Carbonell, Roberto Huezo, Miguel Angel Cerna, (the painter and writer better known as MACLo), Esael Araujo, and many others.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=El_Salvador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Salvadoran_cuisine" title="Salvadoran cuisine">Salvadoran cuisine</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Plain_pupusas_revueltas.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Plain_pupusas_revueltas.jpg/220px-Plain_pupusas_revueltas.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Plain_pupusas_revueltas.jpg/330px-Plain_pupusas_revueltas.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Plain_pupusas_revueltas.jpg/440px-Plain_pupusas_revueltas.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3008" data-file-height="2000" /></a><figcaption><i>Pupusas</i>, the national and most famous dish of El Salvador.</figcaption></figure>
<p>One of El Salvador's notable dishes is the <i><a href="/wiki/Pupusa" title="Pupusa">pupusa</a></i>. <i>Pupusas</i> are handmade corn tortillas (made of <i><a href="/wiki/Masa" title="Masa">masa de maíz</a></i> or <i>masa de arroz</i>, a maize or rice flour dough used in <a href="/wiki/Latin_American_cuisine" title="Latin American cuisine">Latin American cuisine</a>) stuffed with one or more of the following: cheese (usually a soft Salvadoran cheese such as <i><a href="/wiki/Quesillo" title="Quesillo">quesillo</a></i>, similar to mozzarella), <i><a href="/wiki/Chicharr%C3%B3n" title="Chicharrón">chicharrón</a></i>, or <a href="/wiki/Refried_beans" title="Refried beans">refried beans</a>. Sometimes the filling is <i>queso con loroco</i> (cheese combined with <i><a href="/wiki/Loroco" class="mw-redirect" title="Loroco">loroco</a></i>, a vine flower bud native to Central America).<sup id="cite_ref-Elsalvador.com_195-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Elsalvador.com-195">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> <i>Pupusas revueltas</i> are <i>pupusas</i> filled with beans, cheese and pork. There are also vegetarian options. Some adventurous restaurants even offer <i>pupusas</i> stuffed with shrimp or spinach. The name <i>pupusa</i> comes from the Pipil-Nahuatl word, <i>pupushahua</i>. The origins of the <i>pupusa</i> are debated, although its presence in El Salvador is known to predate the arrival of the Spaniards.<sup id="cite_ref-Elsalvador.com_195-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Elsalvador.com-195">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In El Salvador, the pupusa is considered a Mesoamerican ancestral legacy and the most popular dish nationally. It has been designated as the "National Dish of El Salvador" via the Legislative Decree no. 655 in the Salvadoran Constitution. The decree also indicates that every second Sunday in November, the country will celebrate the "National Day of the Pupusas".<sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Two other typical Salvadoran dishes are <i>yuca frita</i> and <i>panes con pollo</i>. <i>Yuca frita</i> is deep fried <a href="/wiki/Cassava" title="Cassava">cassava</a> root served with <a href="/wiki/Curtido" title="Curtido">curtido</a> (a pickled cabbage, onion and carrot topping) and pork rinds with <i>pescaditas</i> (fried baby sardines). Yuca is sometimes served boiled instead of fried. <i>Pan con pollo/pavo</i> (bread with chicken/turkey) are warm turkey or chicken-filled submarine sandwiches. The bird is marinated and then roasted with spices and hand-pulled. This sandwich is traditionally served with tomato and watercress along with cucumber, onion, lettuce, mayonnaise, and mustard.
</p><p>One of El Salvador's typical breakfasts is fried plantain, usually served with cream. It is common in Salvadoran restaurants and homes, including those of immigrants to the United States. <a href="/wiki/Alguashte" title="Alguashte">Alguashte</a>, a condiment made from dried, ground <a href="/wiki/Pepitas" class="mw-redirect" title="Pepitas">pepitas</a>, is commonly incorporated into savoury and sweet Salvadoran dishes. "<i>Maria Luisa</i>" is a dessert commonly found in El Salvador. It is a layered cake that is soaked in orange marmalade and sprinkled with powdered sugar. One of the most popular desserts is the cake <i>Pastel de tres leches</i> (Cake of three milks),  consisting of three types of milk: evaporated milk, condensed milk, and cream.
</p><p>A popular drink that Salvadorans enjoy is <i><a href="/wiki/Horchata" title="Horchata">horchata</a></i>. <i>Horchata</i> is most commonly made of the <a href="/wiki/Morro_seed" class="mw-redirect" title="Morro seed">morro seed</a> ground into a powder and added to milk or water, and sugar. <i>Horchata</i> is drunk year-round, and can be drunk at any time of day. It mostly is accompanied by a plate of <i>pupusas</i> or fried yuca. <i>Horchata</i> from El Salvador has a very distinct taste and is not to be confused with Mexican <i>horchata</i>, which is rice-based. Coffee is also a common morning beverage.<sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> Other popular drinks in El Salvador include <i>ensalada</i>, a drink made of chopped fruit swimming in fruit juice, and <i>Kolachampan</i>, a sugar cane-flavoured carbonated beverage.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=El_Salvador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_El_Salvador" title="Music of El Salvador">Music of El Salvador</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:XXIV_Conmemoraci%C3%B3n_de_los_Acuerdos_de_Paz._(24315595462).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/XXIV_Conmemoraci%C3%B3n_de_los_Acuerdos_de_Paz._%2824315595462%29.jpg/220px-XXIV_Conmemoraci%C3%B3n_de_los_Acuerdos_de_Paz._%2824315595462%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/XXIV_Conmemoraci%C3%B3n_de_los_Acuerdos_de_Paz._%2824315595462%29.jpg/330px-XXIV_Conmemoraci%C3%B3n_de_los_Acuerdos_de_Paz._%2824315595462%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/XXIV_Conmemoraci%C3%B3n_de_los_Acuerdos_de_Paz._%2824315595462%29.jpg/440px-XXIV_Conmemoraci%C3%B3n_de_los_Acuerdos_de_Paz._%2824315595462%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5616" data-file-height="3744" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/%C3%81lvaro_Torres" title="Álvaro Torres">Álvaro Torres</a> is one of the most famous Salvadoran singers mainly in the Latin pop genre, romantic ballads and boleros.</figcaption></figure><p>Traditional Salvadoran music is a mixture of indigenous, Spanish, and African influences. It includes religious songs (mostly used to celebrate Christmas and other holidays, especially feast days of the saints). Other musical repertoire consists of danza, pasillo, marcha and cancione which are composed of parading bands, street performances, or onstage dances, either in groups or paired. Satirical and rural lyrical themes are common. Traditional instruments used are the <i><a href="/wiki/Marimba" title="Marimba">marimba</a></i>, <i>tepehuaste</i>, flutes, drums, <a href="/wiki/G%C3%BCiro" title="Güiro">scrapers</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gourd" title="Gourd">gourds</a>, as well as guitars among others. El Salvador's well known folk dance is known as <i><a href="/wiki/Xuc" title="Xuc">Xuc</a></i> which originated in <a href="/wiki/Cojutepeque" title="Cojutepeque">Cojutepeque</a>, Cuscatlan. Caribbean, Colombian, and Mexican music has become customary listening radio and party in the country, especially boleros, cumbia, merengue, Latin pop, salsa, bachata, and reggaeton.
</p><h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=El_Salvador&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: Sport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sport_in_El_Salvador" class="mw-redirect" title="Sport in El Salvador">Sport in El Salvador</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Monumental_Estadio_Cuscatlan.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/Monumental_Estadio_Cuscatlan.jpg/220px-Monumental_Estadio_Cuscatlan.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/Monumental_Estadio_Cuscatlan.jpg/330px-Monumental_Estadio_Cuscatlan.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/Monumental_Estadio_Cuscatlan.jpg/440px-Monumental_Estadio_Cuscatlan.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3968" data-file-height="2976" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Estadio_Cuscatl%C3%A1n" title="Estadio Cuscatlán">Estadio Cuscatlán</a> in San Salvador is the largest stadium in Central America</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">Football</a> is the most popular sport in El Salvador. The <a href="/wiki/El_Salvador_national_football_team" title="El Salvador national football team">El Salvador national football team</a> qualified for the <a href="/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup" title="FIFA World Cup">FIFA World Cup</a> in <a href="/wiki/1970_FIFA_World_Cup" title="1970 FIFA World Cup">1970</a> and <a href="/wiki/1982_FIFA_World_Cup" title="1982 FIFA World Cup">1982</a>. Their qualification for the 1970 tournament was marred by the <a href="/wiki/Football_War" title="Football War">Football War</a>, a war against <a href="/wiki/Honduras" title="Honduras">Honduras</a>, whose team El Salvador's had defeated. The national football team play at the <a href="/wiki/Estadio_Cuscatl%C3%A1n" title="Estadio Cuscatlán">Estadio Cuscatlán</a> in San Salvador. It opened in 1976 and seats 53,400, making it the largest stadium in Central America and the Caribbean.<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup></p><div style="clear:both;"></div>
